## Backtrack ledger

What was lost, when, and what it cost, so that it is paid once.

**2026-09-07, the same fault twice: the commit that recorded the first
bundle was itself one.** `57b6950` is named as a documents-only commit and
carries the deletion of `src/core/ccl.rs` with it. `git rm` had already
STAGED that deletion; `git add docs/...` added to the index rather than
replacing it, and `git commit` takes the whole index. Staging by path is not
enough — the index carries what was staged before. Paid by committing with
an explicit pathspec (`git commit --only <paths>`), which commits exactly
the named files whatever the index holds, and by reading `git show --stat`
of every commit before writing the next.

**2026-09-07, a bundle: `git add -A` swept the auditor's doc edits into
F2(a).** `75cd02d` is named for one item — three dead `core/` modules
deleted — and also carries the auditor's uncommitted recording of the
persistence ruling (`MU-STANDARDS.md` §10, `MU-ENGINEERING.md` §8, the F4
row). Two items in one commit, which §5.1 returns, and a doctrine change
landing WITH rather than BEFORE the code it governs, which §5.5 returns.
Cost: the ruling's own commit does not exist, so a reader of the log finds
§10 inside a deletion commit and cannot see when it was ruled without
reading the message of the commit before it. Cause: `git add -A` in a tree
another agent is also writing to. Paid by staging BY PATH from here on —
every commit of Stage 8c names its files — and by this row, which is where
the ruling's landing is recorded.

extractor `agree_up_to_gauge` is deleted, not repaired.

| id | task | chapter | files / functions | status | DoD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | Receipts with parents and heights; TUniversal as height; every duration a stroke or height count; `origin_unix`, `unix_now`, `ticks`, `load_or_genesis`, the `tuniversal` file removed; clock gate in `build.rs`; every `_tu` carrier → `_h`, every mind clock → `_k` | 45 | chronometry.rs `Parent`, `KStepReceipt`, `Height`, `frontier`, `precedes`; mesh.rs `genesis`, `epoch`; cognition.rs `height`, `standing_mag`; sdk.rs `shed`; physics.rs; mycelium-server.rs `rtt_window` | **open** | `height_is_one_plus_max_of_prev_and_parents`, `precedes_implies_lower_height_and_concurrency_is_kept`, `frontier_visits_each_receipt_once`, `extent_is_zero_without_admitted_receipts`, `extent_replays_from_the_home_chain`, `rtt_is_counted_in_strokes`; build fails on `SystemTime`/`Instant`/`Duration` in the gated directories |
| 5.2a | **done — the mind admits hypha receipts.** `Parent` was constructed in exactly three places, all of them tests: every production receipt named no parent, so `append` took `1 + max(h(prev))` over an empty parent set and height was the kernel's own K wearing TUniversal's name. 45.2 says height climbs on ADMITTED receipts and nothing was ever admitted. `court::admission` writes 47.1's prefix (`H_genesis ‖ E_origin ‖ prev ‖ K ‖ height ‖ chart ‖ payload address`, length-prefixed and decodable, payload an address and never a digest of frame bytes) and 47.2's checks in 47.2's own order: origin, then K against the signer's head (parent / gap / stale / equivocation), then height re-derivation. Order matters — checking height first reports every gap as a refusal, because the receipts that make a gapped height re-derive are exactly what the back-fill fetches. Step 2 is the caller's and `admit_receipt` is given no key, so no call site can satisfy admission by verifying bytes alone. `Kernel::admit_receipt` queues what admits; `append_step` cites it, and the parents are held rather than dropped when an append refuses them. ## Stage 6 — corpus: ARC is ingested, never implemented

Ruled 2026-09-07. The mission is a recursive linguistic engine that speaks
anastome; ARC-AGI-2 is the instrument that proves it, never a domain the
engine knows. Chapter 35's invariant is the boundary and it is violated
today. The corpus must also become continuous: a finite held-answer set
retires itself, and the two oracle-free demand sources (dialect and
retirement) are what outlive it.

| id | task | rule / chapter | files | status | DoD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | **The cell-coordinate boundary as a gate.** Chapter 35: no function outside `family::{pair_form, speak_rule, orbit}` reads a cell coordinate of a grid. Violated at mycelium-client.rs 28/261/456 (`grid_to_cells`, `embed`), mycelium-server.rs 757 and registry.rs 70/102 (`refine_delta`). Widen `xtask/lint.rs` to fail the build on `arc::` outside the three functions plus the sensor read-outs, and move the server-side and client-side reads behind them | 35, law 11 | xtask/lint.rs, mycelium-client.rs, mycelium-server.rs, registry.rs | **open** | the gate fails on a reintroduced `arc::` import; no cell coordinate read outside the three |
| 6.2 | **The registry holds listings, not `arc::Task`.** `tasks: Vec<(Address, Task, TaskState)>` types the court's work market on one corpus, so a second corpus is a port and not an intake; `Unsolved`/`Settled(n)` is a parallel lifecycle beside the listing lifecycle chapter 27 and MU-ENGINEERING §6 already define. `task_id` (registry.rs 67) already produces the portable form — `TAG_TASK ‖ listing keys ‖ chain address` — so the intake exists; the carrier does not | MU-PRODUCT §1, §7; 27 | registry.rs, cognition.rs, arc.rs | **open** — after 6.1 | a second corpus registers with no change to registry.rs; `unsolved()` is a fiber read, not a filtered count |
| 6.3 | **The benchmark harness leaves the mind.** `SOLVE_BOUNTY`/`SOLVE_FLOOR` branching and `proj_solved`/`uniq_solved`/`solves_paid` on the client stroke line are scoring inside cognition. Speaking a form onto a withheld input is `speak_rule` and stays; deciding and counting whether it was right is the registry's and the court's | law 1, rule 4, THREE_BINARIES | mycelium-client.rs 754–759, 865 | **open** | no solve counter on a client stroke line; the client does not read a verdict code to score itself |
| 6.4 | **The uplink door.** External corpora enter as families through a door the payer funds, so the oracle is the payer and not the hypha. Restore from `/opt/mycelium/src/bin/uplink.rs` | MU-PRODUCT §1, §3 | new `src/bin/` arm | **open** — after 6.2 | a non-ARC corpus produces listings a voice quotes on |
| 6.5 | **Continuous corpus: the two oracle-free sources.** Dialect → translation (13.10, 14, M9) is a demand verified by `∂θ = 1` with no held answer anywhere, whose supply scales with minds × charts rather than corpus size; retirement (3.8, T10, 16.2) is a demand that stays open on a *fixed* corpus until the geodesic, which off the sensor plane is not reachable. Both are unbuilt and both outlive ARC. Retirement currently mints nothing — pair it with a metered-verification rebate so elegance pays | 13.10, 14, 3.8, T10; MU-ECONOMY §3 | 2.3, 2.4, 1.11 | **open** — Stage 2 and 1.11 are the work | a soak with the ARC book exhausted still prices demands |
| 6.6 | **`settle_use` must not pay a confederate.** It refuses `demander == discoverer` but not a colluding pair: A utters a residual, B cites it, A is paid on use. With self-generated remainder as the live demand source this is farmable today. The demander's chain must be one the court docketed from a corpus or a funded listing | MU-ECONOMY §3, 47.3 | economy.rs `settle_use` | **open** | a two-voice wash trade mints nothing |

## Stage 7 — ceilings and footprint

Ruled 2026-09-07, and **not yet in MU-RECURSION**: the spec has no chapter
on the width of a coordinate. Chapter 38's B1 widens the base to
`(r, c, z, t)` and leaves `before, after` at `u8`, so the doc's own
extension plan does not reach this. Stage 7 lands with an amendment to 19
and 38.1 naming the widths and their refusals; a task family with no
chapter is a gap in the spec, not only in the tree.

| id | task | rule / chapter | files | status | DoD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | **The tape truncates silently.** `sentence/tape.rs` writes 18 counts as `len() as u16` — vertices, faces, loops, gauge lengths, contact anchor, chord, face `i`/`j`, depth — with no `u16::MAX` guard anywhere in the file. A form past the ceiling is written short and read back as a different form. Same fault class as the snapshot codec's `.min(1 << 16)` that the surgery already closed, in a second place | rule 11, 19 | sentence/tape.rs | **open** — first of the stage | a count past the ceiling refuses the encode; `form_tape_v4_refuses_an_overlong_count` |
| 7.2 | **Hue is `u8` and the corpus is about to stop being ARC.** `Cell = (i64, i64, u8, u8)` (sentence/chain.rs 18) and every gauge `Vec<(u8, u8)>` cap the label alphabet at 256. ARC has ten; an external corpus need not. Decide the width once, at the sensor boundary, and carry the refusal when a corpus exceeds it | rule 11, 38.1, law 4 | chain.rs, transport.rs, class.rs, pivot.rs, element/, tape.rs | **open** — after 7.1 | a corpus past the hue ceiling refuses at ingest with the count named, never wraps |
| 7.3 | **The client leaks until the kernel kills it — measured, 2026-09-07.** Reading taken off the Cut B+1 soak before it was stopped (`aa959c4`, 8.7 h): client RSS by uptime 0.34 GB @ 1.0 h, 1.2 GB @ 2.3 h, 2.0 GB @ 3.4 h, 2.7 GB @ 5.5 h, 2.6 GB @ 8.7 h — roughly 0.3–0.6 GB per hour, and the plateau at the top is not convergence, it is death: **four of five clients were OOM-killed and restarted** (`The kernel OOM killer killed some processes in this unit`, clients 3 and 5 at 04:11 and 05:33; `NRestarts=1` on 1, 2, 3, 5). The fleet held ~9 GB of 15.6 GB and the box had 303 MB free. Servers are two orders smaller and flat-ish (a 31 MB, b 220 MB, c 81 MB). The earlier soak note's "84–121 MB and rising" was an early sample of the same curve, read before it mattered. This is not a tuning problem: MU-ENGINEERING §11's release gate is "RSS flat against corpus size over hours" and the client fails it by 30×. Suspects, in the order the measurement points at them: nothing sheds while the aperture is unmeasured (soak-C5), so held listings and ideas accumulate for the process's life; A21 made every address a variable-length composite and addresses are the keys of most tries; `Element::form()` memoises a decoded form per element; the language snapshot is rebuilt by `language_from_words` on every generation change and the old `Arc` is dropped only when nothing holds it | MU-ENGINEERING §11, A21 §5, 24.1, 25 | measurement first — a heap profile of one client over one hour — then the sites it names | **open** — first of the stage, ahead of 7.1: a fleet that dies in three hours cannot soak anything | RSS flat against corpus size over hours on every host; no OOM kill in a full soak; the curve recorded here beside this one |
| 7.3a | **done — a mind sheds on its own K.** 45.4 ruled it and 5.1 did not land it: the shed path read TUniversal, which advances only on admitted receipts (45.2), so on a mesh admitting nothing `aperture_tu` was 0 and `now_tu - last_used_tu > 0` was false for every entry — the truncation arm never fired in 8.7 hours. A fleet refusing work could not reclaim what it held, and the two failures compounded into the OOM. The dialect entry already carried `last_used_k` beside `last_used_tu`; the lifecycle simply was not reading it. `touch`, `note_stroke`, `aperture_k`, `shed_except` and `Entity::shed` count the mind's own K; `last_used_tu` stays as standing extent in heights, which is a coordinate and not a lifecycle | 45.2, 45.4, 5.1 | sdk.rs | **done** | `a_mind_sheds_on_its_own_k_though_the_mesh_never_advances`; `expressed_word_survives_shed_within_the_aperture` rewritten into the coordinate the shed uses |
| 7.3b | **done — the contemplation counts its own K.** The same defect at the second carrier, and 45.4 names `retire_on_t` by name. `moved_tu` → `moved_k` (11 sites), `now_tu` → `now_k` (7); `Entity` passes `session.voice().k()`. This also reaches what `shed_unglued` cannot: it drops only bubbles neither glued nor dirty, and an idea that never discharges stays dirty forever — every idea in a fleet whose pivots find nothing — while `retire_on_t` skips only the glued, so with a K that moves it reclaims stale ideas whether or not they closed | 45.4, 25, 5.1 | bubble.rs, sdk.rs | **done** | the suite; the soak reading is the proof and waits on a fleet |
| 7.5 | **partial — the invariant is computable, conserves through genesis, bond and install, and fails at the claim-escrow.** `Treasury.entries` is the third append-only carrier and `LEDGER_EPOCH_DESIGN.md` specifies its compression exactly: present the epoch as the boundary matrix of the transfer complex, reduce to Smith normal form, and closed circulation loops vanish exactly — structure changes, result does not, the same principle as G6 one level down. It cannot be built yet, and the reason is the point: **the fold's whole correctness condition is that conservation holds across it, and there is no conservation check to hold.** MU-ECONOMY §6 requires `supply = genesis + mint − burn` and `supply = Σ balance + Σ bond + Σ escrow`; on the tree `supply()` sums `mint` rows alone (with `saturating_add`), there is no `genesis` kind, no `burn` kind, no `unstake`, and `escrow_award`/`release_award`/`revoke_award` write **no ledger entry at all**, against §2's "every change to a carrier is a ledger entry". So neither equation is computable and `Treasury::audit` does not exist. Also absent: `core::zlinalg` carries `z_rank` only, so SNF is a build, not a call. Landed since: entry kinds `genesis`, `burn`, `escrow`, `release`, `revoke`, `unstake` with their tags and orientations; `Treasury::genesis` (a finite signed allocation as its own kind, so seeded and earned value stay separable per §6); `Treasury::burn` (bond first then balance, per lane, returning what actually left, so §6 holds through a forfeiture — 47.4, R9); `supply()` as `genesis + mint − burn` with **checked** arithmetic returning `None` rather than a saturated number that does not add up; and `Treasury::audit()` checking **both** equations against the journal and the carriers. **The audit immediately caught a real hole and it is pinned as a claim rather than left for a soak**: `enroll` inserts a bond and writes a `stake` row **without debiting anything**, so bond exists that was never issued and `Σ balance + Σ bond + Σ escrow` exceeds `genesis + mint − burn`. §4 has bond as "μ at risk", staked FROM a balance, and §9 lists stake and unstake for voices as not landed. **Landed since (7.5b):** a stake now MOVES value — `enroll` debits the balance it stakes from (§4: bond is μ at risk), stakes only what is held, and `unstake` returns it the way it went; `Kernel::enroll` funds an identity at genesis first and stakes from that, per MU-PRODUCT §4's "a genesis allocation funds our customer voice and bonds our hyphae", so the allocation is a `genesis` row and stays separable from earned value; `Treasury::minted()` reads mint alone, because five tests were asking "was anything minted?" of `supply()`, which now correctly includes seeded value. **The audit then found the next hole on its first live run and it is pinned with its evidence**: `supply [18, 60, 0, 78]` against carriers `[18, 61, 0, 78]` after two ingests — `escrow_award` inserts the claim's price into the `escrowed` carrier having debited nothing. **This one is a ruling, not a slip**: §5 has a REQUESTER escrowing the price, which is funded and conserves, while §4 has an unbonded voice whose "claim is escrowed against its own deflation", which is value that exists only if the court upholds. §6 counts `Σ escrow` as issued supply, so the two readings cannot both stand — either a claim-escrow is funded from a balance, or it is provisional and is not supply until the ruling mints it. MU-ECONOMY needs the ruling before the code moves. Remaining after that: the escrow mutators write their rows (§2's "every change to a carrier is a ledger entry"); `revoke_award`'s slash path still debits `voices.first()` (G4a's fault at a site that pass missed); and SNF itself, since `core::zlinalg` carries `z_rank` only | MU-ECONOMY §2, §4, §6, §9; LEDGER_EPOCH_DESIGN | economy.rs; core/zlinalg.rs | **partial** | `the_audit_holds_through_genesis_mint_and_burn`, `enroll_conjures_bond_and_the_invariant_says_so`; then `audit` green on the settlement paths, then the fold preserves it |

## Stage 8 — decomposition of the monoliths

Ruled 2026-09-07, after the G-set and before Stage 2. The split is already
ruled in `THREE_BINARIES.md` item 1 and has never landed; `sentence/`
(chapter 18.2's `{chain, form, transport, class, pivot, tape, chart}`) is
the pattern to follow. Stage 2's Mayer–Vietoris work lands squarely in the
part that must move to the court module, which is why the split precedes it.

**The rule: no file over 2500 lines.** A file past that is not read end to
end, and the verification rule of Part VII is that the auditor reads the
function and its carriers end to end — grep is not verification. Every
carrier fault of the G-set (a stale mirror in `basis()`, a maximum standing
in for a sum, two entangled items in one diff) was invisible at 8000 lines
and obvious at the function. The threshold is a reading bound, not a style
preference, and it is the one number in this document that is allowed to be
a literal because it measures a person and not the mathematics.

Over the line at `0b94f78`: **cognition.rs 8055, sdk.rs 3975, family.rs
2760.** Under it and watched, in descending order: element.rs 2346,
bubble.rs 2084, sentence/mod.rs 2029, dialogue.rs 1836, alphabet.rs 1801.
At Stage 8b the bound is 800 and the watch list is the gate's own
`AWAITING_SPLIT` table; element.rs is the first file off it (S1).

A split moves code and changes nothing else: no signature, no behaviour, no
test. Each lands with the suite byte-identical in its counts, so a split
that needs a test changed is not a split and is returned.

Candidate modules from the file's own seams: `kernel` (the carriers and their keyed reads), `book` (listings, pricing, quote, rank), `court` (glue, replay, verdicts, torsion), `market` (escrow, settle, room), `snapshot` (the MYC codec), `germinate` (the percept path) | THREE_BINARIES §1, Part VII | server/cognition.rs | **open** — after the G-set | no module over 2500 lines; `-D warnings` green; the suite's counts unchanged |

| 8.4 | **done — the bound is a gate.** `lint_file_length` in `xtask/lint.rs` fails the build on any `src/**/*.rs` over 2500 lines, so the next monolith is refused at the commit that grows it rather than at an audit a month later. **Verified firing** on a deliberately concatenated 4371-line file rather than assumed. Two findings while landing it: the enumeration gate detects test code by scanning for `#[cfg(test)]` **within** a file and tracking brace depth, so a test module split into its own file carries no marker and every gate fires on it — `tests.rs` is exempt now, but checked (the parent module must declare it under `#[cfg(test)]`, so a file merely *named* `tests.rs` is still linted). And a private **method** in one child module is not visible to a **sibling** child, unlike a private field, which is visible to a module and its descendants: 36 methods called across the cuts are `pub(super)`, `cognition`- or `sdk`-wide and no wider. That is a real visibility change, so "a split changes nothing else" holds for the carriers and not for the methods | Part VII, rule 14 | xtask/lint.rs | **done** | the gate fires on a file grown past the bound |

### Stage 21, the phases (ruled 2026-09-22 by the operator: a landing is a phase, §5.1)

The implementor's cadence is a phase per commit, audited as one landing.
Move-only splits are the one exception and stay their own commits (§1.6).
The rows below are grouped into the phases the operator relays; a phase's
commit message names every row it carries and quotes the suite and the
gate. A phase with a row unreproduced is landed in part and says so.

| phase | rows | what the engine can do after it | gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G** (first, alone) | G1 | nothing new; the reading bound holds again so that a landing can be verified at all | `cd xtask && cargo run` exit 0, quoted in every message from here on |
| **H** | H1b, H2b, H4 | a polychrome object is one body AND one cover piece, two bodies meeting at a corner carry a contact face, and every census of 68–69 is re-read against that | the A.14 census on the diagonal; the four censuses beside their old figures in 70.5 |
| **I** | I1, I2, I3 | the fleet prints the one number the doctrine calls decisive, and its phase line says what it counts | a recut whose readings can be read |
| **H3** | H3 | a body's colour seam is a gauge cocycle the engine reads where its 1-skeleton is a tree | `δ⁰θ` read on a polychrome body; the withhold census's `Acyclic` arm asked one more question |


**Ruling on the Phase H reading (2026-09-22, the coordinator, before the
commit).** H1b and H2b together read the corpus at 732 bodies, 389 corner
contacts, 405 connected pieces and `b₁ 62` on the contact graph with 21
scenes carrying a cycle — the first inter-body cycles this corpus has ever
had — while every form's own loop count fell to 0, the cover meets 51 → 0,
the chords 51 → 0, and the last form reads `cycles: unread 21`. This is
what 70.1 measured in advance: the 188 loops were within polychrome
objects, so removing the hue from the base removes them from the
1-skeleton and moves them to the gauge, where H3 reads them. The phase
LANDS AS IT STANDS, with the table recorded in 70.5 under 70.5's own rule
that a collapse is recorded with the number and not the word. Restoring
the per-hue split is refused (it would put fiber data back in the base to
keep a count up), and inventing a transport for an unread contact is
refused (rule 11). Two things are owed with it: (a) the attribution is an
inference and is checked once, by the probe §6.4 permits — the per-hue
split restored in a detached worktree and the loop count read on the same
instrument — and the number written beside the table; (b) the 21 unread
cycles are the finding. The contact graph has 62 cycles and the forms
read 21 of them as unread, so a contact face exists and its transport
refuses — at the light cone, at a pair the face holds no place for, or as
`NotRigid`, and the instrument does not yet say which (H2c). Those cycles
are the first place a holonomy between two bodies could be hard to spell,
which is the one thing 926f3e5 said the corpus never produces, and they
are read before anything is fixed.
